## Part 02 ch04

- [review, chunk 1, unit 1, minor] hindi / अब इसके बाद: atha ataḥ: English says 'Now, therefore' but Hindi 'अब इसके बाद' renders ataḥ as 'after this'. Also vyāpad is singular (Hindi/English plural 'विपत्तियों/mishaps'). Suggested: Owner to decide whether the book-wide Hindi for athāto is 'अब इसलिए' or 'अब इसके बाद'; check siblings.
- [review, chunk 1, unit 3, minor] english / mouth of the yoni: mukheṣu is locative PLURAL (mukha at the end of the compound); the translation takes it as singular 'mouth' of yoni only. The plural fits 'at the openings of basti, flanks, hips and yoni' at least as well. Left as draft; owner may prefer 'in the basti ... and the mouths of ...'. Suggested: observation only (already noted in notes)
- [review, chunk 1, unit 4, minor] text_flags / mṛdumukha: The flag suggests 'mṛdusukha...' while `words` keep mukha; the translation commits to the rare sense 'soft-surfaced' (Hindi 'कोमल सतह वाले'), which is an interpretation of mukha. 'mṛdu-sukha-śiśira' (soft, comfortable, cool) is the likelier sense. Consider marking the translation 'on a conjectural reading' or rendering mukha literally ('soft-faced'). Suggested: owner decision; no patch
- [review, chunk 1, unit 9, minor] english / the juices (rasa) of an unclear substance: The conjectural reading builds a sense ('absorbed the juices of a substance') on only paripīta and -rasa; 'substance' is supplied for the unclear middle (tatva). Keep it as an unclear marker; low confidence is right. Possibly dissolves into something like 'paripīta ... rasāni' with a lost word; nothing firm. Suggested: owner may shorten to: 'the compound describing them is unclear (only paripīta, thoroughly drunk/absorbed, and rasa are legible)'
- [review, chunk 1, unit 10, minor] split / tat śṛṅga siddhasya: A single token tacchṛṅgasiddhasya is split into three separate words instead of one hyphenated compound tat-śṛṅga-siddhasya (rule 2); the tool cannot merge words, so noted only. Also, nyagrodha buds are usually called śuṅga; 'śṛṅga' may be a spelling of that (the glossary tag 'shoot, disputed' is fair). Suggested: observation only
- [review, chunk 1, unit 18, minor] notes / sarasānyasareṇa: Possible alternative parse sarasāny asareṇa (sarasāni + asareṇa) exists; neither word gives a sense, so the unrestorable treatment is right. Not to be built on. Suggested: observation only
- [review, chunk 1, unit 25, error] split / madhuka-payasyā-asura-dārubhiḥ: Likely misparse: payasyā + sura-dāru gives the same string payasyāsuradāru- with no sandhi change, and suradāru is a well-known synonym of devadāru (deodar, Cedrus deodara; probable). 'asura-dāru' is not an attested drug name, and the note 'do not equate with deva-dāru' discards the simpler reading. Both parses fit the print; the sura-dāru parse should be at least the primary one or offered as an alternative in the translation and notes. Needs a human decision (not patched). Suggested: w = madhuka-payasyā-sura-dārubhiḥ; gloss 'suradāru (deodar, Cedrus deodara; probable)'; English 'madhuka, payasyā (identity disputed) and suradāru (probably deodar; the print could also be read asura-dāru, unidentified)'; Hindi 'सुरदारु यानी सम्भवतः देवदारु'

- [review, chunk 1, unit 2, minor] english|hindi / savair / kṣaṇisya (and the other silent restorations of the chunk): NEEDS A HUMAN DECISION. Emendations carried in `words` only: u2 savair→sarvair ("all"), kṣaṇisya→kṣīṇasya ("wasted"), satṛṣṇo sa; u9 idra→indra; u20 arśāsi→arśāṃsi; u27 masikā→maṣikā (covered: u27 already carries the mark); u24 raṃbu (covered: u24 carries the mark); u32 tuddvāha→tuddāha; u34 pramehiṇa→pramehiṇam. Glossary rule: a correction adopted in a translation needs the mark in both languages. Suggested criterion: mark when a consonant or vowel letter is inserted, deleted or replaced so that a different word is read (savair→sarvair, kṣaṇisya→kṣīṇasya, tuddvāha→tuddāha: u2 would then carry "on a conjectural reading" at the end of the sentence, u32 too); do not mark pure anusvāra or diacritic restorations (idra, arśāsi, pramehiṇa, masikā), which are the print's regular habit. Not patched because it is a book-wide policy call. Suggested: Owner to choose; if the criterion above is accepted append "; on a conjectural reading" / "; अनुमानित पाठ पर" to u2 and u32 (both languages).

Also 'wearing a single garment of many kinds (aneka): old, ...' restructures the compound aneka-jīrṇa-vivarṇa-malina-chinna-ārdra-eka-vasana (the printed 'neko' is itself flagged); the colon and 'of many kinds' are interpretation. ## Part 05 ch02

- [review, chunk 1, unit 4, minor] english / koṣṭha (abdomen, bowel): "bowel" is an interpretive narrowing of koṣṭha (lit. inner chamber, belly). Not a draft-only error; see glossary_suggestions. Suggested: koṣṭha (belly, abdominal interior)
- [review, chunk 1, unit 9, minor] text_flags / svādukāthaiḥ -> svādukvāthaiḥ: Suggestion is plausible, but unit 10 also has a kātha-type form (pañcakolakkathair), so the edition may spell kvātha as kātha (काथ) throughout. The flag should say it may be a spelling variant, not only a scan slip. The sense (decoction) is not in doubt. Suggested: append note
- [review, chunk 1, unit 12, minor] notes / GROUPING: Unit 12 is only the lead-in formula "bhavati cātra"; it has no content of its own. Suggest merging it into unit 13 (lead-in line of the first verse), as the draft brief treats such formulas. Suggested: merge 12 into 13
- [review, chunk 1, unit 18, minor] text_flags / liṃpedaṃtastrivṛtayā dvidhākṛtekṣugaṃḍikaṃ: Not flagged: gaṇḍikā is the usual feminine noun (ikṣugaṇḍikā); the printed -ṃ (n.) may be a slip for -āṃ (f. acc. sg.). The draft only notes the gender mismatch. Meaning unaffected. A possible-typo flag (gaṃḍikaṃ -> gaṃḍikāṃ) would be better than silently reading it as neuter. Suggested: add text_flag: src ... ikṣugaṃḍikaṃ, suggest ... ikṣugaṃḍikāṃ, why "possible: f. gaṇḍikā is the usual word"
- [review, chunk 1, unit 27, minor] text_flags / kaphavātarutāṃścānyān -> kaphavātarujaś cānyān (?): The suggested rujaś (nom. pl.) does not fit: the objects of parimārṣṭi are accusative (gadān, anyān). The smallest correction that keeps the grammar is ta -> ja: kaphavātarujāṃś cānyān (ruja, acc. pl.; ज/त are easily confused in the scan). As printed (rutāṃś) the word means "cries", so the draft reading is marked doubtful, which is right. - [review, chunk 1, unit 5, minor] gloss / atikramam: Gloss 'transgression' vs translation 'error/भूल': 'error' is an interpretation. Suggested: earlier transgression / पहले का अतिक्रम
- [review, chunk 1, unit 12, minor] english / not oiled and sweated: Ambiguous: asnigdha-svinna may be 'not oiled and not sweated' (gloss says so) but English reads 'not (oiled and sweated)'. Hindi has the same ambiguity. Suggested: by one not oiled and not sweated (if the gloss is right)
- [review, chunk 1, unit 13, minor] english / with purging: virecanam is accusative, not instrumental: 'with purging' (also Hindi 'विरेचन से') is an import. - [review, chunk 2, unit 42, minor] gloss / hṛd-graham 'heart-seizure': 'Seizure' now names a modern clinical event (fit, attack); the medical-term rule asks for the plain literal sense of graha. Suggested: Prefer 'heart-grasping' or 'heart-gripping' (human choice; also affects the English).

- [review, chunk 2, unit 49, error] split / sarva-aṃga-grahaṇī vs translations: The word row keeps the printed grahaṇī (f. nom. sg.), but the gloss, Hindi and English all follow the suggested emendation -grahaḥ ('a seizing of all the limbs'; Hindi 'सर्वांग का ग्रहण'). The text_flag therefore changes the sense without the words row showing it (rule 6 asks for the corrected form in words). Translating a line of confidence 'low' as a firm reading is also overconfident: grahaṇī is a real word (f. 'that which seizes') and the printed clause may be read differently. Suggested: Human decision: either put the corrected form in `w` (sarva-aṃga-grahaḥ) and keep the flag, or keep grahaṇī and translate it literally ('the seizer of all limbs') with the emendation only in notes.

The word row also calls the first three a separate dvandva. Suggested: Human decision; suggested wording: 'with astringent, sweet and cold smearings, pourings, immersions, foods and drinks'.
- [review, chunk 2, unit 73, error] english / life-blood and raktapitta (also Hindi जीवरक्त और रक्तपित्त; gloss '(rakta)pitta': The printed compound is jīva-rakta-pitta-yoḥ, literally 'of life-blood and pitta' (dual = two items). Suggested: Translate literally ('life-blood and pitta' / 'जीवरक्त और पित्त') and put the haplology suggestion (jīvarakta-raktapitta, cf. unit 74) in text_flags as 'possible'; the gloss should drop the bracket.

- [review, chunk 1, unit 11, minor] english / in a middling doṣa, in one with atisāra: madhyadoṣe and atisāriṇi are both locatives agreeing with the (unstated) patient; reading madhyadoṣe as a noun 'in a middling doṣa' next to 'in one with atisāra' gives two different referents. More natural: madhya-doṣa as a bahuvrīhi, 'in one with middling doṣa, with atisāra'. Suggested: Owner/human decision; suggested: 'in one with middling doṣa, with atisāra (diarrhoea)'; gram of madhya-doṣe then bahuvrīhi

- [review, chunk 2, unit 48, minor] text_flags / ku~rvaṃto -> kurvan: Weak flag, and the draft is internally inconsistent: the printed form kurvanto gives the 16 syllables (the print has a stray candrabindu, कुँर्वंतो), while the correction kurvan gives 14 and breaks the metre, yet `words` already uses kurvan. Metre favours keeping kurvanto (nom. pl.), which then disagrees with the singular subject basti; an alternative is that the subject is not the basti. Owner to decide between w=kurvan (grammar) and w=kurvantaḥ (metre); either way keep the flag as "possible". Suggested: human decision
- [review, chunk 2, unit 48, minor] hindi / करता हुआ ... पीड़ित: Gender/sense: बस्ति is feminine in the other Hindi lines (निकालेगी, करती), and "पीड़ित" reads as "afflicted" while the English says "pressed" (notes keep both open). Suggested: human decision
- [review, chunk 2, unit 53, minor] split / āklama-nāśāt: Probably two words: ā (up to) + klama-nāśāt (ablative, "until the destruction of fatigue"); a noun āklama is doubtful, and the printed व्यजेदाक्लमनाशाच्च reads naturally as vyajet ā klamanāśāt ca. - [review, chunk 2, unit 53, minor] gram / vyajet: Analysis as a denominative of vyajana looks wrong: vyajet is simply the potential of vi-√aj (to fan, drive), the source of the noun vyajana. Sense unchanged. Suggested: 3 sg. pot. vi-√aj; notes: not a denominative
- [review, chunk 2, unit 62, minor] gram / trivṛt-pathyā-kalkam / anulomanam: "m. acc./nom. sg." is inconsistent: kalka in -kalkam can only be accusative if masculine. - [review, chunk 3, unit 94, minor] text_flags / gudo'valupyataiva: The why says 'the usual change of final e before a vowel gives avalupyata iva'. The suggestion (separate the words, read avalupyate iva) is fine; the explanation is not. Suggested: reword the why

Suggested: decision
